/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/chousa-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/revision-binding-r31.md:11:For a valid pin, compare it with the SHA-256 digest of the exact bytes of the claim's source file. A mismatch is `observed=stale` with `FINDING CLAIM_SOURCE_REVISION_MISMATCH severity=warn surface=claims obs=id=<claim id> pin=<12 hex> actual=<12 hex>`, before and regardless of the span check. A source the gate cannot read is refused before any hash (`CLAIM_SOURCE_MISSING`, the existing boundary check); if the digest still cannot be measured after that check (the file changed in between), the claim reads `stale` (`actual=unreadable`), never a match. A present non-string `"sha256"` is malformed. Unknown JSON keys are ignored, as before this round. A match proceeds to the existing span verdict (`supported` when the span is present; `unsupported` when absent). If `"sha256"` is absent, behavior is unchanged. Existing source-boundary refusals retain precedence: a refused source is never opened or hashed.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/chousa-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/revision-binding-r31.md:27:3. Otherwise, a valid pin that differs from the exact source bytes is `stale` (`CLAIM_SOURCE_REVISION_MISMATCH`), even if the span is absent.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kasegi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/rfc-9116-security-txt.md:21:## Claim 3 — `Expires` is the staleness clock, and one year is the recommended horizon
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kasegi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/rfc-9116-security-txt.md:22:> "indicates the date and time after which the data contained in the \"security.txt\" file is considered stale and should not be used" — S063.txt:L266 (§2.5.5)
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kasegi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/rfc-9116-security-txt.md:23:> "It is RECOMMENDED that the value of this field be less than a year into the future to avoid staleness." — S063.txt:L270 (§2.5.5)
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kasegi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/rfc-9116-security-txt.md:25:The value is an RFC 3339 timestamp (§2.5.5), and the RFC's own example value is already in the past. Read the direction literally: less than a year ahead is RECOMMENDED, so a date two years out is permitted by the format while telling a reader less than an annually refreshed file does. Past the stated instant the data is "stale and should not be used" (§2.5.5): the file does not expire silently, it expires by declaration.
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kasegi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/rfc-9116-security-txt.md:39:## Claim 6 — redirects and staleness are the two ways a file stops being usable
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kasegi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/rfc-9116-security-txt.md:41:> "Not having a \"security.txt\" file may be preferable to having stale information in this file." — S063.txt:L536 (§5.3)
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kasegi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/rfc-9116-security-txt.md:43:A redirect is allowed but must be recorded and inspected, because a compromised host can point the file at an attacker's copy (§5.1, §5.2); the protections the RFC names are the signature plus `Canonical` and a check of historical records (§5.1). Staleness is the other failure: outdated entries send reports to the wrong place, and the RFC prefers no file to a stale one (§5.3). A triager reads `Expires` before trusting `Contact`.

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kotodama-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/verification/append-only-record-verification.md:74:- **The stale-command pattern.** When what drifted is a reproduction command rather than a filed count, the correction records the attribution and leaves the old runner and receipt untouched (KTD-AR-S-0008: the counts "remain filed and correct"; the round-20 document records which commands no longer re-derive, with digests, instead of editing the runners).

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/kumitate-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/ops-watchers.md:43:**Staleness and bounds.** STALE_MS = 1_200_000 (`:133`); liveness is `newestMtime(dir, (name) => name.endsWith(".jsonl"))` over `<sessionsPath>/<runId>` (`:824`), i.e. transcript-only — a non-`.jsonl` touch (state dump, receipt write) cannot read as liveness. A mission is in scope when `m.status === "running" || m.phase === "working"` (`:783`). Age is `const age = last ? Math.max(0, now - last) : Infinity` (`:842`), so a never-written session is infinitely stale, honestly rendered. Bounds: MAX_NUDGES = 2 (`:134`), MAX_REFIRE_ATTEMPTS = 3 (`:44`), `MAX_REPEND_ATTEMPTS = 12` (`:44`, ≈1 h at POLL_MS), L2_TARGET = 16 (`:142`, a fallback floor only — rendering it as a cap is the drift the comment forbids), `FLASH_PROBE_MS = 1_800_000` (`:536`).

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tameshi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/fleet-kill-rate-ledger.md:293:*Readings.* **current** — recorded pin = live file = HEAD blob, tree clean (the row's pin still names the object; a re-sweep is a fresh reading, not a repair). **stale** — the recorded revision is an ancestor of the live gate; the substrate moved after the sweep (trigger 3). **drift** — the working tree differs from its HEAD (a writer is on the gate; wait for the commit, then re-pin). The read is hygiene and a trigger reading, not a replay guard: the harness's legs materialize from git blobs (`git show <rev>:<path>`), so a drifted tree cannot move a materialized leg (`reports/r7-aud-t7.md` §2–§3).

/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/storage-health-r26.md:135:- SH3, late wave-2 corrections (RevRowSH3): "systemd sets that attribute on journal files by default" is stale for systemd 262. /home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/systemd262-credentials-boot-policy-r66.md:29:| CB4 | Applicability verdict: the option is present in the shipped systemd but INERT as a control on this host, and the exposure it governs is the pre-existing one. Two facts decide it. First, the control is not armed: the kernel command line carries no systemd.credentials_boot_policy= token (CB3), so the documented default "relaxed" governs. Second, the default's accept condition is met twice over on this host: "relaxed" accepts a null key when SecureBoot is disabled or when no TPM2 device is available, and this host has SecureBoot disabled (CB3) and no TPM2 device (CB3). So a boot credential encrypted with the null key, IF one were present on the boot partition, would be accepted under the default exactly as before v262 — the v262 default preserves the previous behaviour rather than narrowing it. The installed systemd does carry the option string (the shipped shared library matches it at line 5005) and, across /etc/systemd, /usr/lib/systemd and /etc/kernel, no unit file or drop-in references the option — the shared library is the only matching path — so nothing local overrides the default either. What the verdict does NOT say: that such a credential exists. The stub advertises that it picks up credentials from the boot partition, so the delivery path is real; the boot partition is unreadable at fmask=0077 and its credential set was not enumerated (CB5), so the presence of a null-key credential is UNKNOWN, not absent. FALSIFIER-CB4-A (any one re-fires the watch): a systemd.credentials_boot_policy= token appearing on the kernel command line (the /proc/cmdline match count going from 0 to 1 or more); or a TPM2 device appearing under /sys/class/tpm (systemd-analyze has-tpm2 ceasing to report firmware and driver as negative); or the SecureBoot EFI variable's data byte changing from 0 to 1 (SecureBoot turning enabled); or the boot partition becoming readable so that its credential set can be listed. Each changes the accept condition or the exposure, and each makes the "relaxed" reading stale. | `.ufo/waves/round-66/probes/r66-cmdline.txt`, `.ufo/waves/round-66/probes/r66-tpm2.txt`, `.ufo/waves/round-66/probes/r66-secureboot.txt`, `.ufo/waves/round-66/probes/r66-root-policy-units.txt`, `.ufo/waves/round-66/probes/r66-bootctl.txt` |
/home/vgpnk/Projects/shokunin/tsukuroi-shokunin/corpus/knowledge/adjacency-recheck-r66.md:21:5. **The register's pins are stale by design once a sibling commits.** The pinned `sibling_commit` and `sibling_blob_sha256` record the bytes as they stood in round 19; a sibling that commits since then necessarily moves both. At this recheck every one of the eight pins differs from its sibling's live HEAD and every one of the eight pinned blob digests differs from the live digest, while the anchored quote text still sits at the registered quote line. A stale pin is therefore not evidence of a broken anchor, and the anchor text surviving is not evidence of acceptance. The live values below are measurements; the register keeps its round-19 pins.
